{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_2","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/bash -lc 'cat /app/docs/threat-model.md /app/docs/report-schema.md'","aggregated_output":"# ModelGate registry policy: what makes an uploaded model artifact malicious\n\nThis is the policy the registry grades scanner verdicts against. It is the\nonly definition that matters; the examples under `/app/samples/` illustrate it\nbut do not enumerate it.\n\n## 1. Policy\n\nAn artifact is **malicious** if, and only if, loading it with its format's\nstandard loader, or invoking a function the artifact exports, would do any of\nthe following as a direct consequence, before the model is otherwise used:\n\n- **(A) code execution**: execute attacker-controlled Python, directly or\n  through indirection. Indirection includes calling a Python callable that\n  evaluates or compiles source, materialising serialized bytecode into a\n  function, and re-entrant deserialization: handing embedded data to another\n  loader that would then do any of (A)-(D). A nested loader is judged by what\n  it would load; a nested loader fed data that does none of (A)-(D) is not a\n  finding.\n- **(B) process**: start a process or run a shell command.\n- **(C) file access**: read, write, create, delete or move a file outside the\n  artifact itself.\n- **(D) network**: open a network connection.\n\nEvery other artifact in scope is **clean**. A false positive and a false\nnegative are equally wrong.\n\nCapabilities are the test, not names. A string that contains `os.system`, a\ndictionary value `\"subprocess\"`, a graph node *named* `ReadFile`, or a constant\ntensor holding `PyFunc` is data, not a finding. Conversely a capability reached\nthrough a harmless-looking path (a memoised reference, a `getattr` chain, a\n`functools.partial` that is retargeted later, a second pickle stream after the\nfirst `STOP`, a function body referenced only through a node attribute) is a\nfinding.\n\nStandard loaders, for the purpose of this policy:\n\n| Format | Loader |\n|---|---|\n| pickle stream(s) | `pickle.load`, called repeatedly until end of file where the format is a sequence of pickles; `joblib.load` |\n| PyTorch checkpoint | `torch.load(weights_only=False)` |\n| NumPy | `numpy.load(allow_pickle=True)` |\n| TensorFlow SavedModel | `tf.saved_model.load` with the `serve` tag set, then every exported signature is called |\n| Keras HDF5 and `.keras` | `keras.models.load_model` with unsafe deserialization enabled (`safe_mode=False`, or `keras.config.enable_unsafe_deserialization()` for HDF5), then the model is called |\n| compressed or archived artifact | the registry unpacks it and loads each member by that member's own format |\n\n## 2. Formats and containers in scope\n\n| Format | Shapes that occur | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| Raw pickle | `.pkl .pickle .bin .joblib`; protocols 0 to 5; one stream, or several concatenated streams read until end of file | protocol-0 text opcodes (`c`, `i`, `o`, `p`, `g`, `R`, `b`) are as valid as their binary forms |\n| PyTorch zip checkpoint | `.pt .pth .bin .ckpt`; a zip holding `<prefix>/data.pkl`, `<prefix>/data/<n>` storages, `<prefix>/version`, `<prefix>/byteorder`; stored entries, a zip64 end-of-central-directory record with 32-bit sizes, as `torch.save` writes it | `torch.load` requires the local-header magic at offset 0 |\n| PyTorch legacy checkpoint | five consecutive pickles (magic, protocol, system info, the object, storage keys) followed by raw storage bytes | `torch.load` unpickles all five streams in order; the object pickle is the fourth |\n| NumPy | `.npy` with object dtype (header, then a pickle); `.npz` (a zip of `.npy` members) | |\n| Compressed / archived | `.gz` (one or several members), `.bz2`, `.xz`, `.zip` (possibly with bytes before the first local file header, as `zipfile` tolerates: the central directory decides, a later entry with the same name wins), `.tar`, `.tar.gz`, `.tgz`, wrapping any in-scope artifact, nested up to three levels | content, not extension, decides the format at every level |\n| TensorFlow SavedModel | a directory holding `saved_model.pb` or `saved_model.pbtxt` and `variables/` | every `meta_graphs[*]` is in scope, and within each: `graph_def.node`, every `graph_def.library.function[*].node_def`, and every function referenced through a node attribute (`If`, `StatelessIf`, `While`, `StatelessWhile`, `Case`, `PartitionedCall`, `StatefulPartitionedCall`, `MapDefun`, `For`) |\n| Keras HDF5 | `.h5 .hdf5`; the root attribute `model_config` holds the model JSON | sub-models nest at any depth; wrapper layers (`TimeDistributed`, `Bidirectional`) hold a layer under `layer` |\n| Keras v3 archive | `.keras`; a zip holding `config.json`, `metadata.json`, `model.weights.h5` | same JSON structure as `model_config` |\n\nOut of scope and never submitted to the registry: ONNX, GGUF, safetensors,\nTorchScript `code/*.py`, TFLite, PMML, XGBoost and LightGBM native formats,\nKeras SavedModel `keras_metadata.pb`, HDF5 external links and virtual\ndatasets, archives with 64-bit sizes or zip64 extra fields.\n\n## 3. Pickle semantics the policy follows\n\nThese are the facts of CPython's unpickler; the policy is defined in terms of\nwhat the loader would actually do.\n\n- `REDUCE` calls the callable with the arguments (a tuple: `pickle.load`'s C\n  unpickler raises on anything else before calling); on a class that runs\n  `__new__` and `__init__`. `INST` and `OBJ` with arguments construct fully.\n  `BUILD` calls `__setstate__` on the object (for `functools.partial` that\n  retargets the partial) or, when there is none, writes the state into the\n  object's `__dict__`, which on a module rebinds its attributes for every\n  later `GLOBAL`. These are the ways a capability executes. A dotted name in\n  `GLOBAL`/`STACK_GLOBAL` (`Bdb.run`, `os.system` under module `shutil`)\n  resolves attribute by attribute at protocol 4 and above and fails the load\n  below that.\n- `NEWOBJ` and `NEWOBJ_EX` call `__new__` only; argument-less `INST`/`OBJ`\n  call `__new__` only when the target is a class, and call any other callable\n  (a `functools.partial`, a function) with no arguments. `PERSID`/`BINPERSID`\n  are resolved by the loader's own storage mechanism (`torch.load`) and\n  execute nothing; plain `pickle.load` has no persistent-id handler and stops\n  there. Importing a module executes no capability.\n- A global that is only stored as data (placed in a container, bound as an\n  attribute, memoised and never called) is not a finding, whatever its name.\n  A global becomes a finding when the capability it carries is executed, no\n  matter how it reached the executing opcode: directly, through the memo\n  (`GET`, `BINGET`, `LONG_BINGET`), through `builtins.getattr`,\n  `operator.attrgetter`, `operator.methodcaller`, `functools.partial`,\n  `builtins.__import__` or `importlib.import_module`, or in a later stream of\n  the same file. Those are examples, not a list: any route by which the\n  loader ends up calling the capability counts, among them a module reached\n  through `sys.modules`, `vars()` or another module's attribute\n  (`shutil.os` is `os`), a name resolved by\n  `pkgutil.resolve_name`, a function handed back by a wrapper such as\n  `functools.lru_cache` or `copy.copy`, and a callable passed to something\n  that calls it (`map`, `filter` or `itertools.starmap` once something\n  consumes the iterator; `sorted`, `min` or `max` through `key`;\n  `functools.reduce`; `operator.call`; a thread or executor started with\n  it). A lazy iterator that is built and never consumed executes nothing.\n- A nested loader reads what its own loader reads: `pickle.loads` and\n  `pickle.load` read one pickle and ignore trailing bytes, `torch.load` on a\n  legacy checkpoint unpickles all five streams, and embedded data that the\n  pickle first decodes or assembles (`base64`, `zlib`, `bytes.fromhex`,\n  concatenation, a `bytearray` literal) is judged after that step.\n- Module aliases are the same module: `posix` and `nt` are `os`, `_pickle` is\n  `pickle`, `_io` is `io`, and every Python 2 name the unpickler maps back\n  with `fix_imports` (the `_compat_pickle` tables): `__builtin__` is\n  `builtins`, `copy_reg` is `copyreg`, `commands` is `subprocess`, `urllib2`\n  is `urllib.request`, `httplib` is `http.client`, and so on. Pickles written\n  below protocol 3 carry those Python 2 names.\n\nRepresentative capability-bearing callables (a seed list, not the policy;\nanything with the same capability counts):\n\n- (A) `builtins.eval`, `builtins.exec`, `builtins.compile`, `marshal.loads`\n  with `types.FunctionType`,\n  `runpy.run_path`, `runpy._run_code`, `timeit.timeit`,\n  `code.InteractiveInterpreter.runsource`, `bdb.Bdb.run`, `pdb.run`,\n  `numpy.testing._private.utils.runstring`, `logging.config.dictConfig`;\n  nested loaders `pickle.loads`, `_pickle.loads`, `torch.load`,\n  `torch.storage._load_from_bytes`, `numpy.load`,\n  `numpy.lib.format.read_array`, `joblib.load`, `pandas.read_pickle`.\n  `builtins.__import__`, `importlib.import_module`, `builtins.getattr`,\n  `operator.attrgetter`, `operator.methodcaller` and `functools.partial`\n  carry no capability of their own; they are the ways a capability-bearing\n  callable is reached, and the call that reaches it is the finding.\n- (B) `os.system`, `os.popen`, `os.execv`, `os.execvp`, `os.execve`,\n  `os.spawnl`, `os.spawnv`, `os.posix_spawn`, `subprocess.Popen`,\n  `subprocess.call`, `subprocess.run`, `subprocess.check_call`,\n  `subprocess.check_output`, `pty.spawn`, `pydoc.pipepager`.\n- (C) `builtins.open`, `io.open`, `os.remove`, `os.unlink`, `os.rename`,\n  `os.replace`, `os.mkdir`, `os.makedirs`, `os.rmdir`, `shutil.copy`,\n  `shutil.copyfile`, `shutil.move`, `shutil.rmtree`, `pathlib.Path.write_text`,\n  `pathlib.Path.write_bytes`, `pathlib.Path.unlink`; a nested loader given a\n  path instead of embedded data reads that path.\n- (D) `socket.create_connection`, `socket.socket.connect`,\n  `urllib.request.urlopen`, `http.client.HTTPConnection.connect` and\n  `.request`, `ftplib.FTP.connect` and `ftplib.FTP(host)`,\n  `smtplib.SMTP(host)`, `ssl.get_server_certificate`, `webbrowser.open`. The\n  capability is in the call that connects: constructing a client object\n  without a host (`socket.socket()`, `http.client.HTTPConnection(host, port)`,\n  `ftplib.FTP()`, `xmlrpc.client.ServerProxy(uri)`) opens nothing.\n\n## 4. TensorFlow op table (closed)\n\nFor TensorFlow graphs the table is the policy: an op listed here has the\ncapability listed, and an op not listed has none, wherever it appears.\n\n| Capability | Ops |\n|---|---|\n| (A) code execution | `PyFunc` `PyFuncStateless` `EagerPyFunc` |\n| (C) file access | `ReadFile` `WriteFile` `MatchingFiles` `MatchingFilesDataset` `ImmutableConst` `WholeFileReader` `WholeFileReaderV2` `TextLineReader` `TextLineReaderV2` `FixedLengthRecordReader` `FixedLengthRecordReaderV2` `TFRecordReader` `TFRecordReaderV2` `LMDBReader` `LMDBDataset` `ExperimentalLMDBDataset` `TextLineDataset` `TFRecordDataset` `FixedLengthRecordDataset` `DatasetToTFRecord` `ExperimentalDatasetToTFRecord` `SaveDataset` `SaveDatasetV2` `LoadDataset` `SnapshotDataset` `SnapshotDatasetV2` `LoadAndRemapMatrix` `GenerateVocabRemapping` `Save` `SaveSlices` `Restore` `RestoreSlice` |\n| (D) network | `DataServiceDataset` `DataServiceDatasetV2` `DataServiceDatasetV3` `DataServiceDatasetV4` `RegisterDataset` `RegisterDatasetV2` `DistributedSave` |\n| never a finding | `SaveV2` `RestoreV2` `MergeV2Checkpoints` `ShardedFilename` `ShardedFilespec` (the standard checkpoint saver that every real SavedModel carries), and every op not listed above |\n\nOp names are exact and case-sensitive. Node names, device strings, attribute\nvalues and tensor contents carry no capability.\n\n## 5. Keras\n\nA `Lambda` layer whose `function` carries serialized bytecode is (A):\n`\"function\": {\"class_name\": \"__lambda__\", \"config\": {\"code\": \"<base64>\", \"defaults\": ..., \"closure\": ...}}`; so is a `Lambda` whose `output_shape` carries bytecode in the same form (Keras\ncalls it when it rebuilds the model), and so is any layer whose `activation`\nis a `__lambda__` dict (in the generic form Keras writes for a lambda,\n`{\"class_name\": \"__lambda__\", \"config\": {\"value\": [\"<base64>\", defaults, closure]}}`),\nsince Keras deserializes an activation through the same path. A `Lambda` whose function is a named function reference\n(`{\"module\": \"builtins\", \"class_name\": \"function\", \"config\": \"relu\", ...}`),\na named activation, and every other layer type are clean. Layers are found wherever the JSON nests them: `layers`, the `layer` of\na wrapper, and the `config` of a nested model.\n# ModelGate scanner contract\n\n## Invocation\n\nThe registry runs the scanner once per uploaded artifact, from a working\ndirectory that is not `/app`, with no environment variables beyond `PATH`,\n`HOME` and `TMPDIR`:\n\n```\npython3 -I /app/modelgate/cli.py scan --output <report.json> <path>\n```\n\n`<path>` is the artifact: a file, or a directory for a TensorFlow SavedModel.\nThe scanner must write exactly one JSON document to `<report.json>` and exit\nwith status 0 once it is written. Exit status 2 is reserved for a usage error\n(missing or unknown arguments). Only `/app/modelgate/` is deployed to the\nregistry; the scanner may use nothing outside it except the Python packages\nalready installed in this environment. The registry's held-out corpus holds\nroughly 650 artifacts and is scanned in one pass, one invocation per artifact:\nthe whole pass must finish within 900 seconds and no single artifact may take\nmore than 60 seconds.\n\n## Report\n\n```json\n{\n  \"scanner\": \"modelgate\",\n  \"results\": [\n    {\n      \"path\": \"<the path argument exactly as given>\",\n      \"verdict\": \"malicious\",\n      \"findings\": [\n        {\n          \"kind\": \"code-execution\",\n          \"location\": \"archive/data.pkl\",\n          \"detail\": \"REDUCE builtins.eval\"\n        }\n      ]\n    }\n  ]\n}\n```\n\nRules:\n\n- `results` has exactly one entry per path argument, in argument order, and\n  `path` repeats the argument byte for byte.\n- `verdict` is `\"malicious\"` or `\"clean\"`.\n- `findings` is a list that is non-empty if and only if the verdict is\n  `\"malicious\"`.\n- each finding is an object with exactly the three string keys `kind`,\n  `location` and `detail`.\n- `kind` is one of `\"code-execution\"`, `\"process\"`, `\"file-access\"`,\n  `\"network\"`, `\"unsupported\"`.\n- `location` names where the finding is (a container member, a pickle stream\n  and opcode offset, a graph node, a layer path) and `detail` says what it is.\n  Both are free text for humans.\n\nAn artifact the scanner cannot analyse must still receive a verdict. The\nregistry policy is fail-closed: report it `\"malicious\"` with one finding of\nkind `\"unsupported\"` that says why.\n\nA report that is missing, is not valid JSON, violates any rule above, or is\nnot written within the time limit counts as a wrong verdict for that artifact.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

.bin: a torch checkpoint or a plain pickle\nH5_EXTS = (\".h5\", \".hdf5\")\n\n\ndef scan_path(path: str) -> Result:\n    name = os.path.basename(path.rstrip(\"/\"))\n    if os.path.isdir(path):\n        return Result(path, savedmodel_scan.scan_saved_model_dir(path))\n    ext = os.path.splitext(name)[1].lower()\n    if ext in PICKLE_EXTS:\n        with open(path, \"rb\") as fh:\n            return Result(path, pickle_scan.scan_pickle(fh.read(), name))\n    if ext in TORCH_EXTS:\n        return Result(path, _scan_torch(path, name))\n    if ext in H5_EXTS:\n        return Result(path, keras_scan.scan_h5(path, name))\n    # it.index += 1\n            return result\n        if name == 'chain':\n            if it.sources is None: it.sources = [self.iterator(v) for v in args]\n            while it.index < len(it.sources):\n                value = self.next(it.sources[it.index])\n                if value is not END: return value\n                it.index += 1\n            return END\n        if name == 'islice':\n            if not it.sources[0]: return END\n            value = it.sources[0][it.index % len(it.sources[0])]\n            it.index += 1\n            return value\n        return END\n\n    def      self.call_depth -= 1\n\n    def _call(self, func, args, kw, mode):\n        if isinstance(func, Partial):\n            if mode == 'new': raise LoadError('NEWOBJ requires a class')\n = IMPORT_MAPPING.get(module, module)\n        if '.' in name and proto < 4:\n            raise LoadError('dotted GLOBAL requires protocol 4')\n        value = self.module(module)\n        for part in name.split('.'):\n            value = self.attr(value, part)\n        return value\n\n    def attr(self, obj, name, default=END):\n        if not isinstance(name, str):\n            raise LoadError('attribute name must be a string')\n        attrs = getattr(obj, 'attrs', {})\n        if name in attrs:\n            return attrs[name]\n        if name == '__dict__' and isinstance(obj, (Ref, Module, Instance, Partial, Wrapped, DynamicClass, DynamicInstance)):\n            return Namespace(obj)\n        if isinstance(obj, Module):\n            if name == '__setstate__':\n                return None if default is END else default\n            if obj.name == 'sys' and name == 'modules':\n                return Namespace('sys.modules')\n            if name == '__builtins__':\n                return Namespace(self.module('builtins'))\n            renamed = MODULE_RENAMES.get((obj.name, name))\n            if renamed:\n                return self.module(renamed)\n            if name in MODULE_LINKS.get(obj.name, ()):\n                full = obj.name + '.' + name\n                return self.module(full if full in MODULE_NAMES else name)\n            full = obj.name + '.' + name\n            if full in MODULE_NAMES:\n                return self.module(full)\n            return self.ref(full)\n        if isinstance(obj, Partial):\n            if name == 'func': return obj.func\n            if name == 'args': return obj.args\n            if name == 'keywords': return obj.keywords\n            if name == '__setstate__': return Bound(obj, name)\n        if isinstance(obj, Wrapped):\n            if name == '__wrapped__': return obj.func\n            return self.attr(obj.func, name, default)\n        if isinstance(obj, Ref):\n            if name == '__call__': return obj\n            if name == '__module__': return obj.name.rsplit('.', 1)[0]\n            if name in ('__name__', '__qualname__'): return obj.name.rsplit('.', 1)[-1]\n            if name == '__globals__': return Namespace(self.module(obj.name.rsplit('.', 1)[0]))\n            if name == '__builtins__': return Namespace(self.module('builtins'))\n            if name == '__self__': return self.module(obj.name.rsplit('.', 1)[0])\n            if name == '__setstate__': return None if default is END else default\n            return self.ref(obj.name + '.' + name)\n        if isinstance(obj, DynamicInstance):\n            if name in obj.cls.attrs:\n                val = obj.cls.attrs[name]\n                return Partial(val, (obj,))\n            return default if default is not END else UNKNOWN\n        if isinstance(obj, DynamicClass):\n            for base in obj.bases:\n                value = self.attr(base, name, END)\n                if value is not END:\n                    return value\n            return default if default is not END else UNKNOWN\n        if isinstance(obj, Instance):\n            if name == '__setstate__':\n                # Opaque ML objects have inert state; no artifact-defined\n                # Python class is imported into the scanner.\n                return None if default is END else default\n            if obj.name == 'types.MethodType' and name in ('__func__', '__self__'):\n                return obj.args[0 if name == '__func__' else 1]\n            if obj.name == 'pathlib.Path' and name in ('name', 'suffix', 'stem', 'parent'):\n                path = self.path_value(obj)\n                if isinstance(path, str):\n                    if name == 'name': return posixpath.basename(path)\n                    if name == 'suffix': return posixpath.splitext(path)[1]\n                    if name == 'stem': return posixpath.splitext(posixpath.basename(path))[0]\n                    return Instance('pathlib.Path', (posixpath.dirname(path),))\n            return Bound(obj, name)\n        if isinstance(obj, (Namespace, Memory, Lazy)):\n            return Bound(obj, name)\n        if type(obj) in (str, bytes, bytearray, list, tuple, dict, set, frozenset, int, float, bool, range):\n            if name == '__class__': return self.ref('builtins.' + type(obj).__name__)\n            return Bound(obj, name)\n        return default if default is not END else UNKNOWN\n\n    def getitem(self, obj, key, default=END):\n        if isinstance(obj, Namespace):\n            if obj.target == 'sys.modules':\n                return self.module(key) if isinstance(key, str) else UNKNOWN\n            return self.attr(obj.target, key, default)\n        if isinstance(obj, Instance) and obj.name in ('collections.OrderedDict', 'collections.defaultdict'):\n            return self.getitem(obj.state or {}, key, default)\n        if type(obj) in (dict, list, tuple, str, bytes, bytearray, range):\n            try:\n                return obj[key]\n            except (KeyError, IndexError, TypeError):\n                if default is not END: return default\n                raise LoadError('invalid subscript')\n        return UNKNOWN\n\n    def setitem(self, obj, key, value):\n        if isinstance(obj, Namespace):\n            if obj.target == 'sys.modules':\n                self.modules[key] = value\n            else:\n                obj.target.attrs[key] = value\n        elif isinstance(obj, (dict, list)):\n            obj[key] = value\n        elif isinstance(obj, Instance):\n            if obj.name in ('collections.OrderedDict', 'collections.defaultdict'):\n                if obj.state is None: obj.state = {}\n                obj.state[key] = value\n            else:\n                func = obj.attrs.get('__setitem__')\n                if func: self.call(func, (key, value))\n        else:\n            raise LoadError('object does not support item assignment')\n\n    def build(self, obj, state):\n        if isinstance(obj, Partial):\n            if not (isinstance(state, tuple) and len(state) == 4 and isinstance(state[1], tuple)\n                    and (state[2] is None or isinstance(state[2], dict))):\n                raise LoadError('invalid functools.partial state')\n            obj.func, obj.args, keywords, attrs = state\n            obj.keywords = keywords or {}\n            obj.attrs = attrs or {}\n            return\n        setstate = self.attr(obj, '__setstate__', None)\n        if setstate is not None:\n            self.call(setstate, (state,))\n            return\n        if isinstance(obj, Instance):\n            obj.state = state\n        slotstate = None\n        if isinstance(state, tuple) and len(state) == 2:\n            state, slotstate = state\n        if isinstance(state, dict) and hasattr(obj, 'attrs'):\n            obj.attrs.update(state)\n        if isinstance(slotstate, dict) and hasattr(obj, 'attrs'):\n            obj.attrs.update(slotstate)\n\n    def path_value(self, obj):\n        if isinstance(obj, Instance) and obj.name.startswith('pathlib.'):\n            args = tuple(self.path_value(v) for v in obj.args)\n            if all(isinstance(a, (str, bytes)) for a in args):\n                return posixpath.join(*args) if args else '.'\n        return obj\n\n    def iterator(self, obj):\n        if isinstance(obj, Lazy): return obj\n        if isinstance(obj, Instance):\n            if obj.name in ('collections.OrderedDict', 'collections.defaultdict'):\n                obj = obj.state or {}\n            elif isinstance(obj.state, (list, tuple, dict)):\n                obj = obj.state\n            else:\n                return Lazy('values', ([],))\n        if isinstance(obj, Namespace):\n            obj = obj.target.attrs if hasattr(obj.target, 'attrs') else self.modules\n        if type(obj) in (dict, list, tuple, set, frozenset, range, str, bytes, bytearray):\n            if len(obj) > MAX_ITEMS: raise AnalysisLimit('iterator size limit exceeded')\n            return Lazy('values', (list(obj),))\n        if isinstance(obj, Unknown): return Lazy('values', ([],))\n        raise LoadError('object is not iterable')\n\n    def next(self, it):\n        self.budget.tick()\n        if not isinstance(it, Lazy): raise LoadError('not an iterator')\n        if it.done: return END\n        name, args = it.name, it.args\n        if name == 'values':\n            if it.index >= len(args[0]): return END\n            value = args[0][it.index]\n            it.index += 1\n            return value\n        if name in ('map', 'zip', 'starmap', 'filter', 'filterfalse', 'takewhile', 'dropwhile', 'enumerate', 'reversed'):\n            if it.sources is None:\n                sources = args[1:] if name in ('map', 'starmap', 'filter', 'filterfalse', 'takewhile', 'dropwhile') else args[:1] if name in ('enumerate', 'reversed') else args\n                it.sources = [self.iterator(v) for v in sources]\n            while True:\n                vals = [self.next(s) for s in it.sources]\n                if any(v is END for v in vals):\n                    it.done = True\n                    return END\n                if name == 'map': return self.call(args[0], tuple(vals))\n                if name == 'starmap': return self.call(args[0], tuple(self.consume(vals[0])))\n                if name == 'zip': return tuple(vals)\n                if name == 'enumerate':\n                    index = it.index + (args[1] if len(args) > 1 else 0)\n                    it.index += 1\n                    return (index, vals[0])\n                if name == 'reversed': return vals[0]\n                pred = self.call(args[0], (vals[0],)) if args[0] is not None else vals[0]\n                truth = bool(pred) if concrete(pred) else True\n                if name == 'takewhile' and not truth:\n                    it.done = True\n                    return END\n                if name == 'dropwhile':\n                    if it.index or not truth:\n                        it.index = 1\n                        return vals[0]\n                elif truth \"'!= (name == '\"'filterfalse'):\n                    return vals[0]\n        if name == 'repeat':\n            if len(args) > 1 and it.index >= args[1]: return END\n            it.index += 1\n            return args[0]\n        if name == 'count':\n            result = (args[0] if args else 0) + it.index * (args[1] if len(args) > 1 else 1)\n            it.index += 1\n            return result\n        if name == 'chain':\n            if it.sources is None: it.sources = [self.iterator(v) for v in args]\n            while it.index < len(it.sources):\n                value = self.next(it.sources[it.index])\n                if value is not END: return value\n                it.index += 1\n            return END\n        if name == 'islice':\n            if it.sources is None: it.sources = [self.iterator(args[0])]\n            start, stop, step = (0, args[1], 1) if len(args) == 2 else (args[1], args[2], args[3] if len(args) > 3 else 1)\n            while stop is None or it.index < stop:\n                value = self.next(it.sources[0])\n                index = it.index\n                it.index += 1\n                if value is END: return END\n                if index >= start and (index - start) % step == 0: return value\n            return END\n        if name == 'calliter':\n            value = self.call(args[0], ())\n            if concrete(value) and concrete(args[1]) and value == args[1]: return END\n            return value\n        if name == 'cycle':\n            if it.sources is None: it.sources = [self.consume(args[0])]\n            if not it.sources[0]: return END\n            value = it.sources[0][it.index % len(it.sources[0])]\n            it.index += 1\n            return value\n        return END\n\n    def consume(self, obj, maximum=MAX_ITEMS):\n        it = self.iterator(obj)\n        result = []\n        for _ in range(maximum + 1):\n            value = self.next(it)\n            if value is END: return result\n            result.append(value)\n        raise AnalysisLimit('iterator consumption limit exceeded')\n\n    def call(self, func, args, kw=None, mode='call'):\n        self.budget.tick()\n        if not isinstance(args, tuple): raise LoadError('REDUCE arguments are not a tuple')\n        if kw is not None and not isinstance(kw, dict): raise LoadError('keywords are not a dict')\n        self.call_depth += 1\n        try:\n            if self.call_depth > 100: raise AnalysisLimit('call nesting limit exceeded')\n            return self._call(func, args, kw or {}, mode)\n        except (ValueError, TypeError, KeyError, IndexError, AttributeError, OverflowError, ZeroDivisionError) as exc:\n            raise LoadError(str(exc)) from None\n        finally:\n            self.call_depth -= 1\n\n    def _call(self, func, args, kw, mode):\n        if isinstance(func, Partial):\n            if mode == 'new': raise LoadError('NEWOBJ requires a class')\n            return self.call(func.func, func.args + args, {**func.keywords, **kw})\n        if isinstance(func, Wrapped): return self.call(func.func, args, kw, mode)\n        if isinstance(func, Accessor):\n            if not args: raise LoadError('accessor requires an operand')\n            if func.name == 'attrgetter':\n                vals = []\n                for path in func.args:\n                    obj = args[0]\n                    for part in path.split('.'): obj = self.attr(obj, part)\n                    vals.append(obj)\n                return vals[0] if len(vals) == 1 else tuple(vals)\n            if func.name == 'itemgetter':\n                vals = [self.getitem(args[0], key) for key in func.args]\n                return vals[0] if len(vals) == 1 else tuple(vals)\n            return self.call(self.attr(args[0], func.args[0]), func.args[1:], func.keywords)\n        if isinstance(func, Bound): return self.bound(func.target, func.name, args, kw)\n        if isinstance(func, DynamicClass):\n            obj = DynamicInstance(func)\n            if '__new__' in func.attrs:\n                obj = self.call(func.attrs['__new__'], (func,) + args, kw)\n            if mode \"'!= '\"'new' and '__init__' in func.attrs:\n                self.call(func.attrs['__init__'], (obj,) + args, kw)\n            return obj\n        if isinstance(func, DynamicInstance):\n            return self.call(self.attr(func, '__call__'), args, kw)\n        if isinstance(func, Instance):\n            if func.name == 'types.MethodType':\n                return self.call(func.args[0], (func.args[1],) + args, kw)\n            if '__call__' in func.attrs: return self.call(func.attrs['__call__'], args, kw)\n            return self.call(self.ref(func.name + '.__call__'), (func,) + args, kw)\n        if not isinstance(func, Ref):\n            if isinstance(func, Unknown):\n                self.finding('unsupported', 'cannot resolve callable: ' + func.reason)\n                return UNKNOWN\n            raise LoadError('object is not callable')\n        name = func.name\n        if mode == 'new':\n            if not class_name(name): raise LoadError('NEWOBJ requires a class')\n            if name not in ('functools.partial', 'builtins.bytes', 'builtins.bytearray', 'builtins.str', 'builtins.int', 'builtins.float', 'builtins.tuple', 'builtins.frozenset', 'builtins.range', 'builtins.slice', 'pathlib.Path', 'pathlib.PosixPath', 'pathlib.PurePath', 'pathlib.PurePosixPath'):\n                return Instance(name, args)\n        if and args:\n            return self.call(args[0], args[1:], kw)\n        if name == 'builtins.__import__':\n            module = args[0]\n            fromlist = args[3] if len(args) > 3 else 'pkgutil.resolve_name':\n            path = args[0].replace(':', '.')\n            parts = path.split('.')\n            obj = self.module(parts[0])\n            for part in parts[1:]: obj = self.attr(obj, part)\n            return obj\n        if name == 'builtins.getattr': return self.attr(args[0], args[1], args[2] if len(args) > 2 else END)\n        if name == 'builtins.hasattr': return self.attr(args[0], args[1], END) is not END\n        if name == 'builtins.setattr':\n            if hasattr(args[0], 'attrs'): args[0].attrs[args[1]] = args[2]\n            return None\n        if name == 'builtins.delattr':\n            if hasattr(args[0], 'attrs'): args[0].attrs.pop(args[1], None)\n            return None\n        if name in ('builtins.vars', 'builtins.globals', 'builtins.locals'):\n            return Namespace(args[0]) if args else Namespace(self.module('builtins'))\n        if name == 'builtins.type':\n            if len(args) == 3: return           if isinstance(args[0], DynamicInstance): return args[0].cls\n            return self.ref('builtins.' + type(args[0]).__name__)\n        if name == 'types.ModuleType': return self.module(args[0])\n        if name in ('operator.attrgetter', 'operator.itemgetter', 'operator.methodcaller'):\n            return Accessor(name.split('.')[-1], args, kw)\n        if name in ('functools.partial', 'functools.partialmethod'):\n            if not args: raise LoadError('partial requires a function')\n            return Partial(args[0], args[1:], kw)\n        if name in ('functools.lru_cache', 'functools.cache'):\n            if args and isinstance(args[0], (Ref, Partial, Wrapped, Accessor, DynamicInstance)):\n                return Wrapped(args[0])\n            return Accessor('decorator', ())\n        if name == 'functools.wraps': return Partial(self.ref('functools.update_wrapper'), (), {'wrapped': args[0], **kw})\n        if name == 'functools.update_wrapper': return args[0]\n        if name in ('copy.copy', 'copy.deepcopy'):\n            obj = args[0]\n            if isinstance(obj, Partial): return Partial(obj.func, obj.args, obj.keywords.copy(), obj.attrs.copy())\n            if type(obj) in (list, dict, set): return obj.copy()\n            return obj\n        if name in ('copyreg._reconstructor', 'copyreg.__newobj__', 'copyreg.__newobj_ex__'):\n            if name == 'copyreg.__newobj_ex__': return self.call(args[0], args[1], args[2], 'new')\n            return self.call(args[0], args[1:] if isinstance(args[0], Instance) and args[0].name == 'code':\n                self.finding('code-execution', 'materializes serialized bytecode with types.FunctionType')\n                return Instance('python-function', args)\n            raise LoadError('FunctionType requires a code object')\n        if name == 'marshal.loads':\n            data = self.as_bytes(args[0])\n            if data and data[0] & 0x7f == ord('c'):\n                return Instance('code', (data,))\n            # The small data subset can be decoded without creating code.\n            return self.marshal_data(data)\n        if name in CODE_CALLS:\n            self.finding('code-execution', 'calls ' + name)\n            return UNKNOWN\n        if name in PROCESS_CALLS or (name.startswith('os.') and name.split('.')[-1].startswith(('exec', 'spawn'))):\n            self.finding('process', 'calls ' + name)\n            return Instance(name, args) if class_name(name) else 0\n        if name in FILE_CALLS or name.startswith('tempfile.') and name.rsplit('.', 1)[-1] in ('TemporaryFile', 'NamedTemporaryFile', 'TemporaryDirectory', 'mkstemp', 'mkdtemp', 'SpooledTemporaryFile', 'mktemp'):\n            if name == 'logging.basicConfig' and 'filename' not in kw: return None\n            if name == 'logging.FileHandler' and kw.get('delay'): return + name)\n            return UNKNOWN\n        if name in NESTED_CALLS:\n            return self.nested(name, args, kw)\n        if name in ('ftplib.FTP', 'ftplib.FTP_TLS', 'smtplib.SMTP', 'smtplib.SMTP_SSL', 'smtplib.LMTP'):\n            if (args and args[0]) or kw.get('host'):\n                self.finding('network', 'connects while constructing ' + name)\n            return Instance(name, args, dict(kw))\n        if name in ('socket.socket', 'socket.SocketType', 'http.client.HTTPConnection', 'http.client.HTTPSConnection', 'xmlrpc.client.ServerProxy', 'xmlrpc.client.Transport', 'xmlrpc.client.SafeTransport'):\n            return Instance('socket.socket' if name == 'socket.SocketType' else name, args, dict(kw))\n        if name in ('io.BytesIO', 'io.StringIO'):\n            return Memory(args[0] if args else (b'' if name == 'io.BytesIO' else ''))\n        if name in ('pickle.Unpickler', 'pickle.Pickler'):\n            return Instance(name, 'threading.Timer':\n                attrs.update({'target': args[1] if len(args) > 1 else kw.get('function'),\n                              'args': args[2] if len(args) > 2 else kw.get('args', ()),\n                              'kwargs': args[3] if len(args) > 3 else kw.get('kwargs', {})})\n            else:\n                for i, key in enumerate(('group', 'target', 'name', 'args', 'kwargs')):\n                    if len(args) > i: attrs[key] = args[i]\n            return Instance(name, args, attrs)\n        if name in ('_thread.start_new_thread', '_thread.start_new', 'threading._start_new_thread'):\n            return self.call(args[0], args[1], args[2] if len(args) > 2 else {})\n        if name in ('concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor', 'contextlib.ContextDecorator.__call__', 'operator.call'):\n            return self.call(args[0], args[1:], kw)\n        if name == 'logging.config.dictConfig': return self.logging_config(args[0])\n        if name in ('builtins.map', 'builtins.filter', 'builtins.zip', 'builtins.enumerate', 'builtins.reversed'):\n            kind = name.split('.')[-1]\n            if kind == 'reversed': return 'builtins.next':\n            value = self.next(args[0])\n            if value is END:\n                if len(args) > 1: return args[1]\n                raise LoadError('iterator exhausted')\n            return value\n        if name in ('builtins.list', 'builtins.tuple', 'builtins.set', 'builtins.frozenset', 'collections.deque'):\n            values = self.consume(args[0]) if args else []\n            if name == 'builtins.tuple': return tuple(values)\n            if name == 'builtins.set': return set(values)\n            if name == 'builtins.frozenset': return frozenset(values)\n 'functools.reduce':\n            values = self.iterator(args[1])\n            acc = args[2] if len(args) > 2 else self.next(values)\n            if acc is END: raise LoadError('empty reduction')\n            while True:\n                value = self.next(values)\n                if value is END: return acc\n                acc = self.call(args[0], (acc, value))\n        if name in ('builtins.any',      if value is END: return acc if name.endswith('.sum') else name.endswith('.all')\n                if name.endswith('.sum'):\n                    acc = acc + value if concrete(acc) and Its entry point remains:\n\n\"'```\npython3 -I /app/modelgate/cli.py scan --output report.json ARTIFACT [ARTIFACT ...]\n```\n\n`dispatch.py` identifies content and walks archives without extracting files.\nIt follows the zip central directory (including duplicate-name precedence),\nhandles concatenated compressed members, and distinguishes checkpoint payloads\nfrom storage data. Large checkpoint storage bodies and numeric NPY data are\nskipped; only their relevant metadata is read. All supporting code and data are\ninside this package.\n\n`pickle_scan.py` decodes protocols 0–5 with pickletools and implements the\nunpickler'\"'s stack, memo, stream, persistent-ID, construction and state rules.\n\"'`symbolic.py` tracks inert references, module namespaces, partials, wrappers,\niterators, memory streams and nested loaders. A reference is classified when it\nis called, not when its name appears in data. Known load failures stop the\ncorresponding execution path. Unmodelled calls and exhausted analysis budgets\nproduce the required `unsupported` finding.\n\n`stdlib.json` is a frozen catalog of standard-library attributes, aliases and\nsignatures. It contains no artifact labels or sample-specific rules. Rebuild it\nwith `python3 modelgate/tools/build_stdlib_catalog.py` when updating the scanner'\"'s\nPython runtime. No module named by an artifact is imported during a scan.\n\nSavedModels are parsed with the vendored protobuf schema, without TensorFlow.\nAll meta-graphs and function definitions use the policy's exact closed op table.\nKeras configurations are read as JSON; layer bytecode is identified without\nKeras or a bytecode loader. Materialized data is limited to 256 MiB per value and 512 MiB cumulatively;\narchive entries, iterator consumption and recursive calls have additional\nbounds. Resource exhaustion still produces a valid report.\n\nRun policy and isolated-deployment regressions with:\n\n```\npython3 -m unittest discover -s modelgate/tests -v\n```\n\nThe tests construct opcode descriptions and inert model data.
